# Working notes removed from the chapters (not part of the paper)

When a chapter is rewritten for readers, the parts that describe the state of the prototype and of the work leave it: status blocks, source lists with working labels, differences to the project's earlier design, deviations of the prototype from the paper, and open obligations. They are kept here, chapter by chapter, as they stood. The current proof status of every statement is in `paper/inventory.md`.

## Ch. 1 Values, canonical form and identity

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** A value has one canonical encoding and equality is equality of encodings. An entity id is a pure function of an identity key, and a binding never changes once made. Two identities that turn out to denote one entity are merged by an additive record, and the survivor does not depend on the order in which keys arrive.
**Status.** Built: canonical encoding, digests, identity domains, bindings, merge with an order-independent survivor. Tested on examples and on two real captures: the survivor rule (Proposition 1.8). Not built: split, migration of identity domains (B3, E10), an independent check of the encoder. Deviations D1, D4, D5 (resolved), D6 (resolved by a change of Def. 5.9); Ch. 14, 14.8.
**Gate.** 1a (theory): canonical form and identity, on which every digest and every determinism statement rests.
Uses: Ch. 0. Used by: Ch. 3 (graph equality, digests), Ch. 4 (facts), Ch. 11 (seals).
Sources: [R01] findings 3, 6, 7, 8, 24; [R02] section 4.3 (binding rows) and R10; `ARCHITECTURE.md` "Identity and ontology"; ADR-001, ADR-050, ADR-090, ADR-094.

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Finding 3.** Canonical encoding is RFC 8785 plus rules N1 to N8. Fixture digests must be computed, not `sha256:example`.
- **Finding 8.** `IdentityKey.CanonicalizationVersion` leaves the key. The version is recorded with the binding. Key equality is on `(tenant, domain, components)`.
- **Finding 24.** All 64-bit-valued wire fields are decimal strings (Rule 1.2). `SchemaWriter.cs` must stop mapping `long` to JSON `integer`.
- **Findings 6, 7.** The identity catalog needs commands allocate, bind (alias), migrate; merge and split follow Theorem 1.7. Two fixtures are required: two projections resolving one entity, and two domains that intentionally do not.
- **Finding 30.** `IdentityEvidence.Confidence` stays unspecified. It does not influence any binding in this chapter.
- **Rule 1.1** (identifier alphabet) is new and proposed.
- **Gap 3** (identity domains as typed graphs): Def. 1.7 is realized by the `IdentityDomain` node type (Ch. 2) and `IdOf` (Ch. 5).

### Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8).

- **D1.** Instants are nanoseconds in a signed 64-bit integer (years 1678 to 2262) and decimals are 28-digit .NET `decimal`, not unbounded (N1 to N3 allow arbitrary precision). The paper should state a bounded range with a typed error, or the engine should use arbitrary precision.
- **D4.** Bindings are an append-only table written with the master commit, not `Key`, `Identity` and `Evidence` graph nodes. Def. 1.10 is realized, the typed-graph form of Def. 2.12 is not.
- **D5 (resolved).** A `Link` whose right key is already bound to another identity of the same entity type merges the two identities. The survivor is the smallest id minted for any key of the entity, including keys that nothing had used yet (Proposition 1.8); a survivor that depended on arrival order was the cause of D21 (Ch. 6, 6.7). The loser gets an additive forwarding record, `Rep` follows the chain, and bindings are never modified. The engine remaps what it had emitted under the loser: the master graph gets a patch that moves the loser's relationships to the survivor, the telemetry windows of re-keyed relationships move with them, and derived graphs follow through their deltas. Two identities of different entity types are a conflict and the link is ignored. Tests (`IdentityMergeTests.cs`): forwarding chains over 2, 5 and 12 identities are acyclic and finite, every forwarded id decreases, `Rep` is idempotent; a merge changes resolution only for the involved keys and modifies no binding; the survivor is the same for all six orders of three merges and equals the minimum of the own ids of the keys; different types are a conflict and not a merge. On the two real captures every replay counts 35 and 43 merges and 0 conflicts, at every chunk size (Ch. 14, 14.5). Not kept: what each identity had emitted before a merge; the merge is realized by the remap patch and the forwarding record.
- **Entity id form** (not numbered in 14.8). Entity ids are `e:` plus 26 base32 characters of SHA-256 over the JCS of `[tenant, domain, component encodings]`: the preimage of Def. 1.9, truncated to 130 bits. The paper should adopt the truncated form or the engine the untruncated one.
- **D6 (resolved by changing the paper).** Relationship ids are scoped by the output ontology module and not by the graph (Ch. 5, Def. 5.9).

### Obligations

| Id | Statement | Discharged by |

| O1.1 | Encoder conformance: `enc` equals a reference JCS implementation on a generated corpus including numerals in all forms, nanosecond instants, supplementary-plane text and nested lists | property test against an independent encoder, on the contract types |
| O1.2 | Theorem 1.7: survivor stability under concurrent merges in different projections, Proposition 1.8 on generated key sequences (it has one synthetic order case and two captures), split semantics | Q10 of [R03]: two projections link the same keys in different orders, replay with reordered independent inputs, compare final ids |
| O1.3 | E10: binding migration across canonicalization versions | experiment E10 in [R02] |


## Ch. 2 Ontologies as type graphs

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** Ontologies, identity domains, projections and graph spaces are typed graphs over a small closed kernel. They are stored, patched, digested and pinned like data, so only the engine is compiled.
**Status.** Built: the kernel (54 concrete node types, 20 operator kinds), conformance with the kernel checks K1 to K6 and K9 to K11, the catalog graph with import pins and release digests, value roles as ontology data (Def. 2.13), and the editor's palette served from the kernel table. Tested: the kernel conforms to itself, its digest equals the compiled constant, and mutated kernels fail (this discharges O2.1). Proved at gate 1a: compatible changes preserve typing (Theorem 2.5, restated with a corrected table). Not built: the compatibility classifier and migrations (Def. 2.9, 2.10), the checks K7 and K8, cross-platform digests (Q13), the system vocabulary as graph nodes (D4). Deviations D2, D3, D4, D22, D25, D32.
**Gate.** 1a (theory) for conformance and digests; 1b (flexible and expressive) for the meta-graph: this chapter says what is data and what is compiled.
Uses: Ch. 1 (values, digests, identity domains). Forward references: Ch. 3 (graph, `κ_O`, `OntologyEdit`), Ch. 4 (patches, signed deltas).
Used by: Ch. 5, 6, 7, 8, 9.
Sources: [R03] sections 1, 2, 3.1, 5, 6; [R01] findings 5, 12, gaps 1, 2, 4, 8, section 2.5 (related work).

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Gap 1.** The metamodel is data: operators, functions and value kinds remain compiled (a closed kernel), but ontologies, identity domains and projections are catalog graphs. A new operator or builtin function still needs an engine release (Ch. 15).
- **Gap 2.** Definitions are graphs in the catalog graph space, versioned by patches and pinned by releases. `ChangeCause.ConfigurationVersion` becomes a revision reference into `catalog` (Def. 3.10).
- **Gap 4.** Per-project ontologies by modules, imports and extension. Properties are per type through `hasProperty`, with inheritance. The contract's global property list (`Model.cs:114-117`) is dropped.
- **Gap 8 and finding 17.** References between definitions are edges, not strings. Row contracts are `RowType` nodes in adapter modules.
- **Finding 5.** Relationship health is declared on `Calls` and `DependsOn` in the fixtures. A fixture that emits it is required by Ch. 5.
- **Finding 12.** Empty endpoint sets mean any type for all relationship types ([R03] says 0..n with 0 = any). This is broader than the review's "NoType only" option.
- **`OntologyDefinition`, `PropertyDefinition`, `OntologyRef`** are replaced by `Module`, `PropertyType` and the serialized `imports` target. Full table in [R03] section 9.
- **Revision type.** [R03] types `Release.revision` as text. The paper uses the integer revision of Def. 3.8 (finding 2).

### Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8). The last two items have no number there.

- **D2.** `module m @ 1.0.0;` takes an optional version, default 1.0.0, because the grammar had no place for a release version.
- **D3.** `Correspondence.source` and `.target` are `sourceRefs` and `targetRefs` (one property name keeps one kind per module). Canonicalizers `LowerInvariant` and `Trim` are function nodes `canon.LowerInvariant` separate from the scalar functions of the same name. A `NoType` entity type and a `NoType` relationship type exist in `K`. Single-child slots use the segment `slot:#0`.
- **D22.** The kernel gained `ValueRole` (Def. 2.13), `Aggregate.order`, `Traverse.target`, an optional `Fixpoint.relation`, `param` ports with `Binding.value` and a declaration palette. Def. 2.2 counts `ValueRole`, `Retain`, `Lift` and `LiftState` (54 concrete node types); each change moved the kernel digest. Details in Ch. 5 (5.10).
- **D4.** There are no `Key`, `Identity` or `Evidence` graph nodes; bindings are an append-only table (Ch. 1). Def. 2.12 is not realized.
- **D25.** The operator kind `Retain` is the nineteenth (Def. 2.2, Ch. 5, Def. 5.15).
- **D32 (resolved).** The operator kind `Lift` is the twentieth, with the node type `LiftState` and the edges `liftEdge` and `liftVia` (Ch. 5, 5.8).
- **`was`** keeps the old id and references use the id segment of the old name.
- **Release digest** follows Def. 2.8. Adapters declare a row-contract *shape*, not a digest, so a definition change needs no rebuild and activation compares shapes (Def. 5.8 says digest).

### Status line of Theorem 2.2 before the rewrite

Status: a finite check and not a mathematical theorem, **tested** in the prototype. The engine executes it at start-up (encode `K`, compare `digest(K)` with the compiled constant, run `conforms(K, K)`), and the tests add mutations: a kernel with one edge type changed fails conformance (`KernelTests.cs`). This is experiment Q2 of [R03] and discharges O2.1.

### Status line of Proposition 2.7 before the rewrite

Status: (a) and (c) **follow from** Def. 2.6, 2.8 and the kernel table. (b) is an **audit by source search**, done once (at the engine of evaluation 2) and not a test: the type `ValueRole` and the slot `presentation` appear in the kernel table, the ontology view, lowering, the printer, the ontology export and the palette, and nowhere else. **Tested** by example: the served ontology of the deployment health graph carries `Healthy: ok` and `Unhealthy: danger` for `state` and no entry for `name` (`Nothing_about_health_is_in_the_engine_the_state_property_declares_value_roles_the_ontology_serves`); roles parse, print in a canonical order and reparse to the same digest, and an unknown role or an empty list is rejected (`Value_roles_parse_print_and_round_trip_and_reject_unknown_roles`, `DeployHealthTests.cs`).

### Status line of Theorem 2.5 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, restated. Read only, checked by no tool. The first form quantified over a table with three wrong rows and gave a sketch; the counterexamples follow the proof. The classifier that computes the classes is not built, so the theorem says what a classifier must compute and is not evidence about a program: test T-COMPAT ([inventory.md](inventory.md)) compares the classifier with a re-check once it exists (experiment **Q11**). Re-checking stays authoritative.

### Status line of Theorem 2.4 before the rewrite

Status: **proved** (Theorem 1.1, Corollary 1.3, Theorem 3.3). That the digest is reproducible across platforms is experiment **Q13**.

### The worked example before the rewrite

**Worked example: health is an ontology.** The requirement is that health is an ordinary ontology and nothing about it is compiled into the engine. `acme.deployhealth` is that ontology and its projection in one module (`engine/definitions/acme.deployhealth.sattle`), bound in the graph space as the graph `deploymentHealth`:
- Ontology: `Workload` (one node per Deployment, with `replicas`, `readyPods` and `state`) and `Talks` (traffic between the pods of two Deployments, with `state`). `state` declares the roles `Healthy` as `ok` and `Unhealthy` as `danger` on both types.
- Projection `deploymentHealth`: it counts ready pods per Deployment. Every Deployment contributes a row with zero and every ready pod a row with one, combined by `Union` and summed by `Group`, so a Deployment with no ready pod still has a count. `state` is `Healthy` when the count exceeds the parameter `minReady` and `Unhealthy` otherwise. `Talks.state` is the worse state of the two ends, by two `Join` operators over the calls of the master graph. The parameter is `in minReady: param Integer = 0;` and is bound in the space (Ch. 5).
- More requirements are more rules or statements of the projection that set or update the same property. No new operator and no engine change is needed.
- The master graph carries what this needs from the Kubernetes adapter: `Pod.ready`, `Deployment` and `ControlledBy` from pod to Deployment (the ReplicaSet name gives the link).
- Tested (`DeployHealthTests.cs`, parametrized over `minReady` 0 and 1) on the synthetic fixture `deploy-health` (a shop with `web` of two pods and `db` of one): the state goes to `Unhealthy` when the last ready pod goes and back by retraction when it returns, one patch per change, and the edge follows the worse end.
- Measured on the real captures (Ch. 14, 14.4): 22 `Workload` and 38 `Talks` on the first capture, 22 and 36 on the newest. One Deployment (one replica, no ready pod) is `Unhealthy` on both, and on the first capture the two `Talks` edges into it are `Unhealthy`.

This section maps statements to the tests that exist. Test names are from `engine/tests/Sattle.Tests/`. "B" means 4,000 seeded random cases per property. "C" means example or fixture tests.

## Ch. 3 Graphs, revisions and the graph space

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** A graph is a finite map from element keys to states. It changes only by guarded edits that are committed in a linear revision log, so history is immutable and a revision is reproducible. A relationship may name an endpoint that the graph does not hold, and such a reference is explicit data.
**Status.** Built. Tested as properties on 4,000 seeded cases each: well-formedness, immutability of history, equality by digest. Revisions are integer positions, written as strings on the wire. Not built: the telemetry watermark of Def. 6.8 in the frontier.
**Gate.** 1a (theory): graphs, revisions and the log.
Uses: Ch. 1 (values, `enc`, `digest`, entity ids). Used by: Ch. 4, 7, 11, 13.
Forward references: Ch. 2 (ontology conformance, Def. 2.6), Ch. 5 (graph definitions).
Sources: [R01] findings 1, 2, 3, 25, section 2.1; [R02] D1 to D16; `ARCHITECTURE.md` "Master graph and composition", "The graph data structure"; ADR-052 to 055, 065, 083, 084.

### Definition 3.14 before the rewrite (with protocol and evidence names)

**Definition 3.14 (reference).** A *reference* of `G` is an entity id that is an endpoint of a relationship of `G` and is not in `dom G`. In a graph space a reference is in one of two states: `inOtherGraph`, when another graph of the space holds the entity (the master graph first, then the others in their order), or `absent`, when no graph does. A reference carries the identity key its id was minted from (Ch. 1), so a missing endpoint is not an opaque id: a reader can say what the relationship points at even though nothing was ever recorded about it. A dangling relationship is thus a statement about coverage, "an observation names something no source described", and not an error. The engine serves the references of every snapshot (`extent.references`) and a count per relationship type, end and state for every graph (`danglingBy`; protocol version 3.11, `engine/PROTOCOL.md`). Since version 3.12 the references are also served alone and paged (`GET /graphs/{g}/references`), and `replay --out` writes them as one file per graph. Measured on the newest real capture at the engine of evaluation 12 (`paper/evidence/health-head16.txt`): the master graph has 590 references, all `absent`, one node, 563 Azure resources that the Activity Log names and Azure Resource Graph never listed (6,420 `Concerns` relationships) and 26 pods that flows or warning events name and Kubernetes never listed (140 `Calls` ends, 71 `About`); the derived graphs have none. Tests are listed in the appendix (Artifact, A.8).

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Finding 1.** `RelationshipRef(ProjectionId, RelationshipId)` becomes `(GraphId, RelationshipId)`. The relationship uniqueness index is keyed by `(tenant, graph, source, target, type)`. "Projection" never denotes an output.
- **Finding 2.** `Revision(string)` becomes an integer position with successor. The string is an encoding at the boundary only.
- **Finding 3.** Graph equality and digests use Def. 3.5 on canonical encodings (Ch. 1).
- **Finding 25.** The snapshot seal includes the input frontier (Ch. 11).
- **Finding 26.** Virtual regions, `VirtualRegionEdit` and symbolic patches are outside the core model (Ch. 0). `OntologyEdit` is an ordinary edit on `κ_O`.
- **New.** Telemetry-role properties are excluded from element states (WF4). The ontology slot has its own key.
- **Finding 17 and 19** (one input binding list at graph-space level, unreachable operators) belong to Ch. 5.

### Obligations

| Id | Statement | Discharged by |
|---|---|---|
| O3.1 | `apply`, commit rule and `U` hold on the contract types, with relationship generators (retype, dangling endpoints) | property tests on the contracts (finding 34) |
| O3.2 | F1 holds for the engine | Ch. 6, benchmark B4 and the determinism tests of Ch. 14 |


## Ch. 4 Change algebra

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** Two structures describe change and are kept apart on purpose. Patches are ordered, partial and guarded, and every patch has an inverse. Signed deltas are commutative and total, and may not denote a graph. A homomorphism connects them. Editing definitions is the same algebra: an edit is a patch on a draft and undo is the inverse patch.
**Status.** Built. Tested as properties: Theorems 4.1 to 4.9 on 4,000 seeded cases each, including retype, removal and guard-only edits (Ch. 14, 14.6). Tested on examples only: drafts and undo (4.6), in the engine and in the canvas editor. Proposition 4.11 (two views) is proved under the premise RT of Theorem 5.6. The contract types of iteration 0.1 are not implemented; the engine has its own.
**Gate.** 1a (theory): the change algebra.
Uses: Ch. 1 (canonical form), Ch. 3 (graph, edit, apply, revision). Used by: Ch. 6, 8, 9, Ch. 11.
Sources: [R01] findings 11, 21, 33, 34, 35, T1 to T4; [R02] D7 to D14, L8, L9, L10; `ARCHITECTURE.md` "Changes and arithmetic".

### Status line of Proposition 4.5 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, restated at the second reading of gate 1a: the first form called the structure an inverse category without the zero and a groupoid without `net`, and both are false as stated. *Proof.* A patch acts on graphs as the partial injection `G ↦ apply(p, G)` (Theorem 4.3), and the action determines the patch: its domain fixes the keys and before states, its values the after states. `p then q` is defined iff the composed partial injection is defined on some graph (take `G` with the before states of `p` and, on the other keys of `q`, the before states of `q`), and then represents it (Theorem 4.2). So patches with the zero are a set of partial injections closed under composition and inverse, and in the partial injections of a set composition is associative, inverses in the stated sense are unique, and the idempotents are the partial identities, which commute. For the restriction: the net form of a composed segment from `i` to `j` is `toPatch` of `χ(G_j) − χ(G_i)` (Theorem 4.8), a function of the two states (Theorem 3.1), and the net form of a segment followed by its inverse is the empty patch. ∎ (Theorems 4.1 and 4.3 are the two facts used; the restriction is by Theorem 3.1, since `G_j` is determined by the log.) The categorical framing of patches follows [mimram2013]. The statements of [R01] section 2.1 and [R02] L9 ("groupoid") hold only in the restricted sense.

### Status line of Proposition 4.10 before the rewrite

Status: **proved** from Theorem 4.3 and Def. 3.7 for (a) and (c), by the definition of `apply` for (b). Tested as an example on the engine and the client: `Structural_edits_print_canonically_and_undo_is_an_inverse_patch_not_a_rewind`, and in the canvas the tests "applies edits to the draft and the browser copy, undo and redo are inverse patches", "a new edit clears the redo stack", "keeps 99 steps" and "a rejected patch changes nothing and reports the engine's code". The tests are examples. There is no generated edit sequence on drafts (O4.3).

### Status line of Proposition 4.11 before the rewrite

Status: **proved under the named premise RT** (Theorem 5.6), restated. RT is a property of the printer and the parser, tested on the example modules and wanted on generated graphs (test T-ROUNDTRIP, [inventory.md](inventory.md)). A draft with a missing required slot or reference is reported as `Unprintable` with the element id: the graph view is a superset of the text view. The first form was conditional on `parse(print(G)) = G` for every well-formed graph, which Theorem 5.6 no longer claims.

### Note after Theorem 4.4 before the rewrite

A canonical serialization order of edits is still required for digests and signed artifacts. It is by `(kind, id)` with `κ_O` first. This supersedes ADR-004 ("independent changes may vary in replay order") for the encoding, in agreement with ADR-074 (finding 21).

### Mapping to the contracts

In the contracts of iteration 0.1 a C# type error already prevents mixing patches and signed deltas. The operations map as follows.

| Paper | contract of iteration 0.1 | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `p then q` | `PatchThen` | defined iff Def. 4.2, else `AlgebraRejected` |
| `inverse(p)` | `PatchInverse` | |
| `δ(p)`, `toDelta` | `ConvertPatchToDelta` | |
| `toPatch` | `ConvertDeltaToPatch` | needs `DeltaAtBase`, target version, ontology |
| `squash` | `SquashPatches` | returns `LossReport` |
| `z₁ + z₂`, `−z` | `DeltaAdd`, `DeltaSubtract`, `DeltaNegate` | |

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Finding 11.** `then` and `inverse` replace "addition" and "negation" for patches. "Endpoint swap" is replaced by "swap of `from` and `to` revisions".
- **Finding 21.** Edits are a set; canonical serialization order is `(kind, id)`. ADR-004 is superseded for encoding purposes.
- **Finding 35.** The oracle must assert "`q then p` is undefined" and prove associativity of `apply` composition (Theorem 4.2), not list concatenation.
- **Def. 4.2 retains guard-only edits.** The oracle and `ARCHITECTURE.md` drop `(k, b, b)`. Dropping makes definedness non-associative: after `p` edits `k` from `b` to `a` and `q` edits it back to `b`, a following `u` with a wrong before state on `k` would compose with `(p then q)` but not with `q`. `net` and `squash` drop guards explicitly and report the loss.
- **Proposition 4.5.** Patches are an inverse category, groupoid only on a log segment. The review's word "groupoid" is refined.
- **Finding 33.** The oracle currently tests stand-ins (tuples, sets). Obligation O4.1 moves the tests to the contract types.
- **Symbolic patches** (`DiffProfile.Symbolic`) are out of scope (Ch. 0, finding 26).
- **Definition editing (new against iteration 0.1).** The contracts say nothing about editing definitions. Protocol version 3 edits them as patches on a draft overlay with undo as inverse patch (4.6). The 99-step undo stack, the module-level rebase rule and the canonical text returned with every answer are engine and client decisions recorded here.

### Obligations

| Id | Statement | Discharged by |
|---|---|---|
| O4.1 | Theorems 4.1 to 4.3 and 4.7 to 4.8 hold on the contract types, including relationship edits with retype, dangling endpoints and the ontology slot | property tests on `GraphPatch` and `SignedDelta`; at least 10,000 generated cases per property, reported as properties, not assertion counts (finding 34) |
| O4.2 | Canonical edit order `(kind, id)` is used by every encoder and digest | conformance test against the Ch. 1 corpus |
| O4.3 | Proposition 4.10 on generated draft edit sequences (patches, undo, redo, rejected guards) | seeded property tests of draft editing, in the style of the tests of the change algebra |


## Ch. 5 Projection language

### Status block, sources and opening

**Claim.** A projection is a typed pipeline of operators from a closed set and is checked before it runs. Telemetry cannot influence structure except through a crossing operator. A projection that summarizes a graph is a higher level of abstraction over it: it lifts the edges together with the nodes (5.8).
**Status.** Built: 19 of the 20 operator kinds of Def. 2.2 (`Match` without quantified paths, `Filter`, `Map`, `Window`, `Group`, `Series`, `Rollup`, `Threshold`, `Join`, `Traverse`, `Fixpoint` as a bounded unrolling, `Values`, `Union` with the policy `preserveEvidence`, `Distinct`, `Retain`, `Lift`, `EmitEntity`, `EmitRelationship`, `Link`), parameters of projections (Def. 5.14), aggregates declared per context in `GET /palette` (Def. 5.6, D26), quantities and units for numeric parameters (a duration is nanoseconds on the wire and `24h` in text), refactoring (5.7), and levels with lifted edges written with the `lift` statement, which is lowered to existing operators (5.8). Tested on examples: typed errors with positions, refactoring on one module, retention on fixtures and both captures, the aggregate matrix at the checker, edge lifting and the `lift` statement (28 and 36 cases, run in set A at evaluation 10; set A is runs of the project that the authors did not repeat, Ch. 14, 14.1). Proved on the model: the properties of lifting (Propositions 5.10 to 5.12) and retention (Proposition 5.9). Proved under named premises at gate 1a, each restated with counterexamples to its first form: type soundness (Theorem 5.2, premise KS, with runtime faults local to their row, Def. 5.19), lossless syntax on canonical graphs (Theorem 5.6, premise RT), refactoring (Proposition 5.7, premises R0 to R5, of which the engine checks two). Not built: `Delta`, the `Union` policies `requireEqual`, `preferFirst` and `preferLast`, rule class `sync` (Ch. 8). Deviations D2, D5, D6, D11, D13, D22, D25, D26, D32.
**Gate.** 1b (flexible and expressive): the language is the subject of that gate, and the chapter does not yet state what the language can and cannot express. 1a (theory) for its typing theorems and the properties of lifting.
Uses: Ch. 1 (identity), Ch. 2 (kernel, closure, conformance, telemetry declaration), Ch. 3 (graph space), Ch. 4 (patches).
Used by: Ch. 6, 7, 8, 9.
Sources: [R03] sections 2.3, 2.5, 3, 5, 7, 8; [R01] findings 4, 13 to 20, gaps 1, 5, 8.

A **projection** is a definition: a typed graph in the catalog (Def. 2.1). Its output is a derived graph (Def. 3.11). This chapter fixes the structure of the definition, the type system that makes every accepted definition checkable at load time, the identity expressions, and the concrete text syntax. The semantics (what a definition denotes, and its incremental form) is Ch. 6. The direction classes of rules are Ch. 8.

### Definition 5.9 before the rewrite

**Definition 5.9 (identity expression, relationship key).** An emit's `id` is either a reference to a matched entity (reuse its timeless id, ADR-001) or `IdOf(d, c̄)`, typed by E-Id. `IdOf` resolves the key `(t, d, c̄)` through the binding set (Def. 1.10) or mints `alloc(ik)` (Def. 1.9). An aggregate output is a new entity in a domain whose components are the group keys. It never reuses a member's identity (ADR-094). Membership is an ordinary output relationship, not an identity edge. An optional component makes the `IdOf` optional, and the consuming emit skips the row.
A relationship emit names a **key rule** `κ_r` (the `key` property). The relationship id is `rid(o, κ_r, s, t) = base32(H(JCS([tenant, o, κ_r, s, t])))`, where `o` is the module of the output ontology of the projection. **Neither the type nor the producing projection or graph is an input.** A retype keeps the id (ADR-086), and moving an emit to another projection or graph of the same output ontology keeps it (Proposition 5.7). Two graphs of one ontology that hold the same key rule and endpoints hold the same id, which is harmless because uniqueness `U` (Def. 3.3) is per graph.
Cross-domain equivalence is evidence, not union. `Link` emits an `Evidence` node with `left` and `right` edges to two `Key`s under an `EquivalencePolicy` between two domains. In mode `assert` with confidence at least the threshold the evidence is accepted at once and a `sameAs` edge is materialized; in mode `review` it stays `proposed` until a configuration decision. An accepted `sameAs` between keys bound to different identities is a merge in the sense of Theorem 1.7 (smaller-id survivor, `mergedInto` forwarding). Splits only by explicit decision. `IdentityEvidence.Confidence` is a decimal in `[0,1]` compared with the threshold and is otherwise uninterpreted (finding 30).

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Finding 4.** Relationship identity is a key rule name plus endpoints (Def. 5.9). `RetypeCollision` becomes `retype ∈ {reject, keepExisting, takeNew}` (Def. 5.10). `IdentityRule` as a free string is replaced by `IdOf` with a declared domain (gap 8).
- **Finding 13.** Field paths are `FieldRef` with `path` of the form `f` or `v.p`, typed by E-Prop.
- **Finding 14.** Port signatures are kernel data (Def. 5.2, K9). The unused `Port` record is the kernel `Port` node.
- **Findings 15 and 16.** One fixture per operator is required, including relationship emission. Recursion in `Fixpoint` goes through the internal ports `rec` and `next`, and transitive closure is the admitted fixture (Ch. 6).
- **Finding 17.** One binding list at graph-space level: `Binding` nodes (Def. 5.8). Row contracts are `RowType` nodes.
- **Finding 18.** There is no `Outputs` list. The sinks are the emits, and their facts are unioned into the single output graph under the uniqueness rule and P2 ownership.
- **Finding 19.** An operator with no consumer is a warning (W5). A sink unreachable from an input is an error.
- **Finding 20.** `Scaffold` is replaced by the `Values` operator feeding emits. Scaffold entities are keyed by identity expression, not raw ids.
- **Gap 5.** Direction is declared per rule (Ch. 8).
- **Entity id derivation.** [R03] section 5 derives ids by `e:` plus base32 of SHA-256 over the concatenation of tenant, domain id and length-prefixed components, truncated to 26 characters. Def. 1.9 hashes the JCS array, untruncated. Either is a pure injective-up-to-collision function (Theorem 1.4). A choice must be made once and fixed in the encoder specification. Truncation to 26 base32 characters (130 bits) raises the collision bound.
- **Relationship id scope.** [R03] hashes the projection id and finding 1 of [R01] proposed the graph id. Def. 5.9 hashes the module of the output ontology, as the engine does, so that a refactoring keeps ids (Proposition 5.7, D6).
- **Refactoring.** Extract, inline and move rule are new in protocol version 3 (5.7). The contracts have none.
- **Closed language.** Adding an operator, function or value kind remains an engine release (Ch. 15).

### Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8).

- **D5 (resolved).** A `Link` whose key is already bound to another identity of the same entity type merges the two identities; different types are a conflict and the link is ignored (Ch. 1, Theorem 1.7, Proposition 1.8).
- **D6 (resolved by changing the paper).** The engine hashes `[tenant, output ontology module, key rule, source id, target id]`. The paper now does the same (Def. 5.9). Confirmed by the project.
- **D11.** `Join`, `Traverse`, `Fixpoint`, `Values`, `Union`, `Distinct` and the aggregates `min`, `max`, `mean`, `weightedMean`, `any`, `all`, `last` are built. Their typing rules have negative example tests (clashing join columns, a join without an equality, a bad traverse depth, an unknown traverse start, a fixpoint step with other columns, `Values` rows of different shapes or with a non-constant, `Union` of unequal rows). `Delta` parses or checks to a typed error with a position and is `supported: false` in `GET /palette` with the reason: no property in the definitions or the captures is cumulative, so it would ship without data to test it on. `Union` has only the conflict policy `preserveEvidence`, because rows have no key; the other three policies are `supported: false` for the same reason. `Match` has no quantified paths. The fixpoint typing condition F5 (Ch. 6) is not checked: the engine bounds the unrolling instead (Ch. 6, D11).
- **D13.** `v.p` read columns are not carried past `Group`, `Rollup` or `Threshold`. This agrees with the typing rules (Group outputs keys and aggregates only).
- **D22.** The kernel gained `ValueRole` (Ch. 2, Def. 2.13), an optional `order` slot on `Aggregate`, a `target` property on `Traverse`, an optional `Fixpoint.relation` (inferred from the seed), ports of kind `param` with a `valueKind` and a constant `Binding.value`, and a palette that describes the declarations of a module (protocol 3.1 and 3.2). Each changed the kernel digest. The paper's Def. 2.2 and 5.3 did not have them. Where the new aggregates may be used over telemetry is D26.
- **D25.** The kernel gained the operator kind `Retain` ([R03] had 18 kinds, and the retention of evidence was only a question, O9.8). It has a `timestampField` and an `age`, ports `in` and `out`, and changed the kernel digest. It is a generic operator, not a property of an ontology: the three evidence rules of `acme.ops` read their rows through `|> retain on time for 24h`. The engine README records a design alternative that was not taken, a retention declared on the entity type of an ontology, because it would have removed elements by a second mechanism next to the support counts. Its `age` is a duration with a quantity and a unit in `/palette`, and the canvas editor takes `36h` (Ch. 12, 12.4).
- **D26.** The engine decides where an aggregate may be used and says so in `/palette`: `count` in `Group` and `Rollup`; `countDistinct`, `any`, `all`, `weightedMean`, `last` in `Group` over data only; `sum`, `min`, `max` in `Group` over numeric data and in `Rollup` over numeric telemetry that is not cumulative; `mean` in `Group` and in `Rollup` over a gauge only. Def. 5.6 differs in two places: it allows `weightedMean` and `last` over a `gauge` (the engine has them for data only, so a `Rollup` with a weighted mean is a typed error), and it allows `mean` over `delta` (the engine requires a gauge). The engine's rule is the stricter one and answers the open point of D22 about the temporality of the new aggregates. Tests: every aggregate in a `Group` over data is accepted exactly when the palette lists `Group`, and in a `Rollup` over a delta value exactly when it lists `Rollup` without a `gauge` requirement. A test runs the whole matrix of `sum`, `min`, `max` and `mean` over a delta, a gauge and a cumulative value through the checker and compares each outcome with what the palette declares (a cumulative value is refused by every one, with a message that says to apply `Delta` first; `mean` over a delta is refused with "use sum" and accepted over a gauge; `count` has no requirement). These tests are at the checker: a `Rollup` over a gauge or a cumulative value is accepted or refused, and its values were not evaluated on data in a test.
- **D32 (resolved).** A level was written as about twenty ordinary statements and nothing required the image to be a function. The `lift` statement replaces them (5.8): it is an operator kind of the kernel that the planner lowers to the same operators, and the checker requires the mapping to be declared a function or the statement to carry an `images` policy. Cardinality is still not checked at ingest (O5.8).
- **Syntax.** The text syntax is the grammar of [R03] with the optional module version (D2). Errors print as `file:line:col: error Code: message`.

### Obligations

| Id | Statement | Discharged by |
|---|---|---|
| O5.1 | Premise KS of Theorem 5.2, and (a) and (b) on generated projections | test T-SOUND: property tests on the checker and a reference evaluator |
| O5.2 | Premise RT of Theorem 5.6, and which well-formed graphs are canonical | test T-ROUNDTRIP, Q1, Q8 |
| O5.3 | Every operator has a fixture and a negative fixture per judgment | conformance corpus (Ch. 14) |
| O5.4 | Compatibility between `Series` over another graph's telemetry and replay | closed-window records in the input frontier (Ch. 6, Def. 6.8) |
| O5.5 | Proposition 5.7 on generated projections; R3, R4 and (for `moveRule`) R5 checked by the engine or reported as not preserved | test T-REFACTOR: extract a random selection, commit, compare `Out`; one negative case per premise |
| O5.6 | Relationship id scope (Def. 5.9) confirmed by the project. Open: whether `o` is the module name or its digest | identity corpus of Ch. 1 |
| O5.7 | Def. 5.6 replaced by the per-context rule of D26 (or the engine extended to `weightedMean` and `last` over a gauge), and F5 as a checker rule | checker tests with `mean` over each temporality (done) and an arithmetic fixpoint body; values of a `Rollup` over a gauge evaluated on data |

Discharged: the `lift` statement with a printer round trip; the checker rule that the mapping is a function or the statement has an `images` policy; `Realizes` declared `0 .. 1`; a stated policy for `Backs` (`images each`). Open: the cardinality checked at ingest; Propositions 5.10 to 5.12 and the lowering on generated graphs and images; composition over more than two levels | a cardinality check on rows as they are ingested; property tests with random graphs and images that compare a lift with Def. 5.17 and 5.18 directly |


## Ch. 6 Semantics and incrementality

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** The meaning of a projection is a function of its recorded inputs. Incremental evaluation computes that function, the result does not depend on how the input is cut into batches (under the premises B1 to B4), and a replay reproduces the revision log.
**Status.** Built: a vectorized interpreter over interned columns with an incremental strategy per operator, support-counted element layers, ordered evaluation with one atomic commit per graph, `Join` as a hash join with a bilinear delta, `Traverse` as bounded reach, `Fixpoint` as a bounded unrolling and not semi-naive iteration (D11). Tested: T6 and T7 on fixtures and on both real captures, where the state digest is one value at chunk sizes 1, 50, 777, 100,000 and the default (Ch. 14, 14.5); B1 and B2 by unit tests that fail when their fix is reverted; B3 on examples, with no generator of arrival orders; T9 and T10 on an acyclic example only. Proved relative to two cited theorems: T5, with cases corrected at gate 1a (`Match`, operators filtered by the frontier, `Threshold` as a fold of the recorded sequence, materialization). T6 has a fourth premise B4, found by deriving its proof again. Not built: `Delta`. Deviations D7, D9, D11, D14, D17 (resolved), D21 (resolved), D33, D34, D37.
**Gate.** 1a (theory): incremental evaluation equals batch evaluation, batch invariance, determinism. The same results are the argument that gate 1c (efficient) builds on: work proportional to the change.
Uses: Ch. 1 (identity allocation), Ch. 3 (graph, log, frontier, clocks), Ch. 4 (patches, signed deltas, group), Ch. 5 (operators, typing, closed language), Ch. 7 (closed windows).
Used by: Ch. 7, 8, 9, 11, 13.
Sources: [R01] T5 to T10, findings 16, 21; [R02] L4; [R03] sections 3.3, 10, 11; ADR-004, 009, 017, 018, 048; [budiu2023] and [mcsherry2013] for incrementalization.


### Status line of Theorem 6.1 before the rewrite

Status: **proved** by structural induction on the plan, relative to two theorems of [budiu2023] that are stated in the proof and not re-proved (their statements were checked against the arXiv text 2203.16684 at gate 1a), for the operators the engine has. Read only, checked by no tool. A second reading at gate 1a added the cases `Match`, the operators filtered by the frontier and materialization, which the first proof did not list, removed a circular reference between the `Retain` case and Proposition 5.9, and replaced the `Threshold` case, which was wrong for the semantics of Def. 7.10. Two things are outside the proof. (1) An unbounded `Fixpoint` needs the nested construction of [budiu2023] Section 5 and [mcsherry2013]; the engine has only the bounded unrolling, which the proof covers. (2) That the code of each strategy of 6.4 is the rule proved here is a property of the implementation: obligation **O6.1**, tested through T6 on fixtures and both captures, and wanted as a generated test per operator (test T-INC, [inventory.md](inventory.md)).

### Status line of Theorem 6.2 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, given T5 and the four premises below. The first version of this theorem stated none of them. The prototype violated the first two on one real input (D17) and the third on another (D21). The fourth was found by deriving the proof again at gate 1a: Def. 7.10 states the requirement and the theorem did not list it.

### Status line of Theorem 6.3 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, given the premises F1 (Theorem 3.5), Proposition 5.4 and Theorem 6.1. The two sentences on batches and on the engine release were added at the second reading of gate 1a; the first form could be read as promising equal commit records for any cut of the same records, which Theorem 6.2(b) excludes.

### Status line of Theorem 6.5 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, under the finite-domain condition F5 (stated in the remark below), by the ascending chain argument on a finite lattice; read only, checked by no tool. With `maxIterations ≥ |A|^k + 1` and `maxFacts ≥ |A|^k` evaluation never hits a limit. **What the engine guarantees is less:** its `Fixpoint` is an unrolling with a bound `max` between 1 and 64, so it always terminates, and it returns `lfp(f, seed)` only when the least fixpoint is reached within `max` steps. F5 is not checked, and the tests use acyclic inputs only. Wanted: test T-FIX ([inventory.md](inventory.md)).

### Status line of Theorem 6.6 before the rewrite

Status: (a) **proved** from Theorem 6.1, (b) **proved** by a counterexample; read only. Neither is tested on a cyclic input (test T-FIX). An earlier part (c), that efficient maintenance of recursive views under retraction is known, was a pointer to the literature and not a statement of this paper; it is now the remark after the proof. Its cost on the engine is not measured.

### Evidence for the premises before the rewrite

*Evidence for the premises.*
- **B1** is tested: `Two_spellings_of_one_resource_id_count_into_one_window_whatever_the_batching` writes one resource under two spellings of its id in one batch, in two batches and in two batches in the other order, and asserts one window with the value 5. With the fix reverted (the group keyed by the raw id) all three cases fail (`paper/evidence/mutation-head6.txt`).
- **B2** is tested: `A_row_for_a_window_beyond_the_late_horizon_is_dropped_and_counted_and_never_replaces_what_was_emitted` (four cases of late rows and lateness) and, from the other side of the horizon, `A_row_for_a_window_inside_the_late_horizon_revises_it` (two cases). With the drop disabled the four cases fail and the two still pass.
- **B3** is tested on examples and on two captures, not in general. Tests check the merge laws and the survivor under all orders of three merges and of three keys (the candidates include the own ids of the keys). Another test has one synthetic input in two arrival orders (the pod row before the flows that name the pod, or after them, so that the flows bind a provisional identity and the pod row merges it) at chunk sizes 1, 2, 5 and 2,000 with equal digests, and it asserts that the merge count differs between the two orders, so the test is not vacuous. The replays of both captures at five chunk sizes give one digest each, with the same merge count at every size (35 and 43; Ch. 14, 14.5). No generator produces random key arrival orders and compares the final ids. Proposition 1.8 states the property with a sketch of its proof.
- **B4** is exercised by the differential harness, which cuts the log of every world at every position and compares with a reference that folds the sequence; whether one of its worlds puts a change of structure and an advance into one step is to be confirmed with set B (test T-B4, [inventory.md](inventory.md)).
- B1 to B3 are also exercised together by every replay of a capture at several chunk sizes and by `LiveReproducibilityTests` with shuffled arrival and random flush points.


### Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Finding 16.** The admitted fixpoint fixture must read its own relation through the internal `rec` and `next` ports. Transitive closure is the fixture (Ch. 5). The oracle currently uses a hand-written closure.
- **Finding 21.** Canonical edit order `(kind, id)` (Theorem 4.4); ADR-004 is superseded for encoding.
- **Telemetry in the frontier.** `gin[g']` carries `(revision, telemetry watermark)` (Def. 6.8). New relative to [R03] and to the contract's `InputFrontier`.
- **Fixpoint condition F5.** New proposed typing rule (Theorem 6.5, Remark).
- **Revised windows** are recomputed per key (Prop. 6.8). [R03] says revised windows are processed "like any other Z-set change", which does not define `Threshold`, an order-dependent operator.
- **Finding 33.** The standing-query test (O6.1) replaces the stand-in tests as evidence for T5.

### Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8).

- **D7.** Windows close inside the projection by `Group` over `Window` and by explicit `Advance` inputs, and replay recomputes closed windows from the raw rows. Closed-window records are not recorded inputs (Def. 7.7). Determinism holds in replay because the raw rows are the recorded input.
- **D9.** On a live source closed windows stay revisable for 3 window widths, then their state is dropped; a recorded capture, replayed or served, keeps 1,000,000 (Ch. 7, 7.7). Def. 6.7 and Prop. 6.8 have no horizon.
- **D11.** `Join`, `Traverse`, `Fixpoint`, `Values`, `Union`, `Distinct` and the aggregates `mean`, `weightedMean`, `any`, `all`, `last` are built, each with example tests that include retraction. `Delta` is not built. `Fixpoint` is a bounded unrolling with `max` levels, not the semi-naive iteration of Def. 6.9: Theorem 6.5 applies when `max` is at least the longest derivation, and a smaller `max` gives a prefix of the closure (the test with `max` 1 on a chain of four nodes derives 5 of the 6 pairs). Unrolling is acyclic, which is why the counterexample of Theorem 6.6(b) does not arise here. The tests cut and restore the middle edge of an acyclic chain. No test retracts on cyclic input, so 6.6(a) has acyclic evidence only. The health model uses `Traverse` to depth 16 for its reach (Ch. 7, 7.5) and not `Fixpoint`. `Join` has no cost measurement (H14).
- **D14 (fusion).** Fusion is by selection vectors and shared column arrays, not generated code. There is no IL or delegate compiler, only the vectorized interpreter (Q14).
- **D33 (resolved).** The engine evaluated `Threshold` over the windows in the look-back only, so a state changed because a window grew old. Since evaluation 12 it folds over the episode with a carried state (Def. 6.7, Def. 7.10), and the differential harness agrees with the reference evaluator.
- **D34 (resolved except for the report).** For conflicting fragments the engine let the last sink and, inside one sink, the last arrival win. Since evaluation 12 the outcome is the function of the fragment multiset of Def. 6.4, and conflicts are counted per run. A commit does not yet list its conflicts.
- **D37 (a `Group` is not re-keyed on an identity merge).** Theorem 6.1 gives the batch result, in which every identity reference resolves through the binding set after the input, so the rows of two keys of one entity are one group once the keys are linked. The engine keys a `Group` by the values on its rows and resolves identity at the sink. After a merge two groups stay two groups, and rows that arrive later with the survivor's id open another. At the sink the contributions of the two groups meet on one element: equal values are one value, and different values are a property conflict (Def. 6.4), so a count or a sum per entity is absent where the batch result is the count or sum over both keys. This is a function of the recorded sequence and the same under every chunking (premise B4), but it is not the batch result. It does not arise in a graph that reads another graph through `Match`, because there a merge arrives as a change of the input graph and the rows are retracted and inserted again. The differential harness has no world with a `Group` keyed by an identity that merges, so it does not see the deviation. Reported by set B (runs of the project that the authors did not repeat; Ch. 14, 14.1); the telemetry side is D37 in Ch. 7.
- **D17 (resolved): the premises B1 and B2.** On the first real capture, chunk size 50 gave a different state digest than the other sizes: one Activity Log telemetry window had the value 2 and not 3, and all graph states were equal. There were two causes. (1) The Activity Log writes one resource under two spellings of its id (`resourcegroups` and `resourceGroups`). The `Group` keyed by the raw text made two aggregates for one element and window, the telemetry store keeps the last one written, and so the value depended on emission order. That violates B1; the definitions now group by `lowerInvariant(resourceId)`. (2) A row for a window whose state had already been dropped, beyond the late horizon, created a new window that replaced the value emitted earlier. That violates B2; such a row is now dropped and counted. This paper found the difference and not the causes; set B found both. Since the fix the digest is equal at every chunk size (`paper/evidence/chunk-50-defect.txt` before, `replay-chunks-head.txt` after).
- **D21 (resolved in two steps): the premise B3.** After D17, chunk size 1 on the first capture still gave a different digest: eight `Calls` relationships of the master differed in one endpoint id, with their telemetry, and the identity counters differed (0 conflicts at chunk size 1, 6 at the larger sizes; `paper/evidence/chunk-1-defect.txt`). The cause was "first binding wins": which of two keys of one entity bound first depended on the chunking. Step one merged two identities that denote one entity. The digest was then equal at five sizes, but the number of merges still depended on the size (1 at 50 and above, 0 at 1; `replay-chunks-merge.txt`), a sign that the final ids were not yet independent of order. Step two made the representative the smallest id minted for any key of the entity (Proposition 1.8). Since then each capture has one digest and one merge count (35 and 43) at all five sizes (Ch. 14, 14.5). The ids of pods changed with each step, so digests from before and after a step are not comparable.
- **Quine invariant.** The T6 test checks that the integrated output equals the final graph and the output under other chunkings, on fixtures and the real capture. It is not a property over random operator sequences (O6.1 remains).

| O6.3 | Theorem 6.3 | replay test: two runs, equal commit records and seal digests (E6, E16) |

## Ch. 7 Telemetry plane

### Status block and sources

**Claim.** Telemetry is a second plane: series on graph elements that never create a revision. Telemetry reaches structure only through closed windows and a threshold with hysteresis, which bounds revision churn and keeps replay deterministic. The health model of 7.5 is the worked example: a master graph of Azure and Kubernetes data summarized into components with lifted dependencies and a roll-up along them.
**Status.** Built: telemetry puts, window closing by logical time, late revision and late drop, partial windows (from Hubble `lost_events` and from the forwarder's loss markers), plane separation, hysteresis, and the operators `Series`, `Rollup`, `Group` and `Threshold`. Tested on examples: plane separation, hysteresis, late revision, partial windows. Measured on a real capture: 235,858 flows and 175,265 VNet flow tuples give 674 master revisions and 9,047 telemetry samples in the master (4,595 samples before telemetry support was counted per row). Proved at gate 1a on the model: series identity under graph change (Law 7.5, restated) and the roll-up (Proposition 7.7). Not built: `Delta`, distributions, closed-window records as recorded inputs (D7), the ADX tiers. Deviations D7, D8, D9, D17 (resolved), D31, D33 (resolved), D35, D36, D37.
**Gate.** 1a (theory) for the telemetry plane. Section 7.5, the health model on the real captures, is early evidence for Phase 2 (gates 2a and 2b).
Uses: Ch. 1 (values), Ch. 3 (graph, revisions, clocks, frontier), Ch. 4 (facts, patches).
Forward references: Ch. 2 (series declarations are `PropertyType` declarations, Def. 2.11), Ch. 5 (crossing operators are `Series`, `Rollup`, `Delta`, `Threshold`, Def. 5.11), Ch. 6 (T7 is Theorem 6.3, cross-graph reads Def. 6.8). Forward to Ch. 11 (storage of series).
Used by: Ch. 11, 13.
Sources: [R01] section 4.1 (T-a to T-f); [R02] sections 1.5, 2.4, 4.9; ADR-010, 014, 021, 022, 023, 028, 065; `ADAPTERS.md`.


### Status line of Theorem 7.1 before the rewrite

Status: the first sentence is **proved**. **The second sentence is false as stated, and its restatement is pending**, for the reason given under Theorem 5.3: a projection can derive graph data from the existence of closed windows without a crossing operator, and the bound of Theorem 7.4 does not cover that path. It is restated, or made true by a typing rule, together with Theorem 5.3.

### Status line of Theorem 7.3 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, given T7 (Theorem 6.3), role non-interference (Theorem 5.3) and the pinned watermark for cross-graph reads (Def. 6.8, Proposition 6.7).

### Status line of Law 7.5 before the rewrite

Status: **proved**, restated. Item 3 follows the principle of Def. 6.4: materialization is a function of the multiset of contributions, never of order. Items 1 and 2 follow from Def. 7.3, 7.9 and 3.6, item 3 from Theorem 7.2. Read only. The first form called items 1 and 2 design rules and gave item 3 without a proof; an intermediate form left case (ii) open. The engine side is not tested: tests with retype and removal fixtures are wanted (O7.4), and on a merge the engine adds the two windows for every property (D36), which is case (i) for `sum` and wrong otherwise; what the engine does today is deviation D37, and the test that is wanted is in the inventory.

### Status line of Proposition 7.7 before the rewrite

Status: **proved** on the model, from Def. 7.12 and Theorems 5.3 and 7.4; read only. That the graph `health` of the engine holds the state of Def. 7.12 is not part of the proposition: it needs that the definition text computes Def. 7.12 (a `Traverse` to depth `D`, Proposition 7.8, and a `Group` with `max`) and Theorem 6.1 for those operators, and it is tested on one synthetic world (a degraded callee, its workload, a workload three edges away, one that does not depend on it, and recovery) and wanted on generated topologies (O7.7). The relation `A` contains lifted edges, whose lowering is tested and not proved (test T-LIFT); the proposition takes `A` as given.

### Status line of Proposition 7.8 before the rewrite

Status: **proved** (breadth-first search to depth `D` visits the nodes at distance at most `D`; a shortest path in a graph of `n` nodes has at most `n - 1` edges). Test of the operator: `Traverse_reaches_to_the_depth_and_follows_edge_node_and_property_changes` (directions `out` and `either`).

### Remark after Theorem 7.3 before the rewrite

*Remark.* This is stricter than the formulation "a projection pins a telemetry frontier and reads series" in [R01] (gap T-c), and it differs from [R03] section 10, where `Series` reads "the closed windows in `[frontier − within, frontier)`" of the telemetry plane and windows close by logical-time controls. The three agree if a series read is defined as a read of the closed-window records up to the pinned watermark (Def. 6.8). [R02] additionally allows raw samples to be lost under overload (ring `DropOldest`), which is why the closed records are recorded and not recomputed from raw samples. Query-through to external stores (Ch. 11) never feeds a structure-emitting projection.

### How the open items of the review are resolved

| Gap ([R01] 4.1) | Resolution |
|---|---|
| T-a telemetry not addressable | Def. 7.3: series keyed by `(g, k, p, τ)`. Read ports in Ch. 11 |
| T-b projections cannot read telemetry | Closed-window records as inputs and crossing operators (Def. 7.7, 7.10) |
| T-c determinism undefined | Def. 7.6 (closing by logical time), Def. 7.11, Theorem 7.3 |
| T-d keep the useful split | Theorem 7.1: samples create no revision; only crossing operators cross, and emit patches |
| T-e ontology support | Def. 7.1: kind, unit, owner types, retention, late policy |
| T-f series identity under change | Law 7.5 |

| Open point of ADR-023 | Answer |
|---|---|
| Granularity | Window widths declared per series, aligned grid (Def. 7.1, 7.5) |
| Delivery guarantees | At-least-once, duplicates removed by `(k, p, τ, i)` (Def. 7.2) |
| Retention | Tiers and resolutions in the declaration (Ch. 11) |
| Ordering against diffs | Closed-window records carry ingestion sequence numbers like any input (Def. 7.7) |
| Queryability | Element view (Def. 7.9) and series reads (Ch. 11) |

Consistency with ADR-014 and ADR-028: telemetry-only inputs do not enter per-element retained history and cause no graph change or diff. A closed-window record that changes graph data through a crossing operator is an input that changed graph data and is retained like one.

### Delta to iteration 0.1

- `TelemetryObservation` gains an observed time and a window width, both as decimal strings (Rule 1.2). Without them series cannot be ordered or rolled up ([R02] section 1.5).
- `EvaluationInput` gains closed-window records. `EvaluationProposal.Telemetry` stays for derived series.
- `PropertyDefinition` for role Telemetry gains the series declaration (Def. 7.1) and an owner type list. The fixture `ex/ontology.json` declares `bytes` and `requests` with no owner type. They must be attached to `Calls`, and `packets` (used by `mapping-scenarios.yaml`) declared (finding 29, gap T-e).
- `IProjectionEngine.ObserveTelemetryAsync(Tenant, Projection)` is replaced by a graph-scoped series read (Ch. 11).
- Windowed aggregate and threshold operators (crossing operators) are added to the operator kernel (Ch. 5, Def. 5.11). The oracle currently rejects any projection that emits a non-GraphData property (`validate_schemas.py:90`). This stays correct for emit and is extended with a telemetry input and crossing operators.
- Findings 15 and 16 (unexercised operators) apply to the new operators: one fixture per operator.

### Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8).

- **D7.** Closed-window records are not recorded as separate inputs. Replay recomputes them from the raw rows, which is deterministic. Theorem 7.3 and Def. 7.7 assume recorded records because the live ring can drop samples. A live run therefore needs either recorded closed-window records or raw rows that are never dropped. The live input takes the second way: it records every envelope before it processes it (Ch. 10, Proposition 10.7).
- **D8.** The telemetry port gains `MarkPartial` and a `Deleted` flag on samples (window retraction), both on the ingest side. Def. 11.4 should include them: they realize `Partial` and the retraction of a revised window.
- **D9.** On a live source a closed window stays revisable for 3 window widths. Beyond it a late row is dropped and counted (`late rows dropped`) and never revives the window, which is premise B2 of Theorem 6.2. `replay` keeps every window revisable by default (`--late-horizon WINDOWS`, default 1,000,000), so that a finite replay drops nothing, and a served capture uses the same horizon (D31); `serve --live` keeps 3. Def. 7.6 has no horizon and should get one, stated on logical time.
- **D31 (resolved).** A served capture used the live horizon of D9, which dropped 192,095 old-timestamped rows of the newest capture and changed the health states (9 `Healthy` and 2 `Unknown` in place of 7 `Healthy` and 4 `Unhealthy`, set B). A served capture now uses the replay horizon.
- **D33 (resolved).** `Threshold` was evaluated over the look-back and not over the episode with a carried state (Def. 7.10; Ch. 6, 6.7); fixed at evaluation 12.
- **D36.** On an identity merge the engine adds the windows of the two ids for every property; Law 7.5, item 3, asks for the combination by the property's combine rule and for no value with a counted conflict where two different stored values are not monoid values. Right for `sum` only.
- **D37.** Reported by set B after the ruling of Law 7.5, item 3. The engine combines a window that both ids have by `sum`, `min` or `max`, and under any other rule keeps equal values and removes the value of different ones. It differs from the ruling in four places: "no value" is not yet absorbing across a third id; the conflict is not reported, the window is only removed; a mean with sum and count and a last with its order do not exist as stored pairs, so every `mean` and `last` falls under case (ii); and two rows that reach one window after the link through two groups meet under the rule of premise B1 (equal: one value, different: no value) and not under the combine rule, so for `sum` the engine gives no value where the as-if value is the sum. The reference evaluator has the last behaviour too, so the differential harness does not see it.
- **D35.** The telemetry store drops windows 48 h behind the newest window it has seen, without a declaration and without saying so. Def. 7.13 is the intended rule.
- **Operators.** `Delta`, quantiles and distributions are not built. `Rollup` supports `sum`, `min`, `max` and `mean` over gauges.
- **Real capture.** No partial window and no lost event occurred in either capture, so `Partial(sourceLoss)` and the forwarder reasons are exercised only by fixture tests.
- **D17 (resolved).** On the first real capture one of the 4,595 master telemetry samples (an Activity Log operation count of one window) had the value 3 with chunk size 100,000 and 2 with chunk size 50. The two causes, one resource id grouped under two spellings and rows that revived windows beyond the late horizon, and their fixes are in Ch. 6 (6.7). They became the premises B1 and B2 of Theorem 6.2.

### Obligations

| Id | Statement | Discharged by |
|---|---|---|
| O7.1 | Monoid laws of Def. 7.8 and the roll-up equality of Theorem 7.2 | property tests with exact arithmetic, generated sample sets including resets and duplicates |
| O7.2 | The compiler admits telemetry into structure only through `Series`, `Rollup`, `Delta`, `Threshold`, and window operators carry a declared late policy | Theorem 5.3 (proved), checker tests of P4 and the temporality algebra (O5.1) |
| O7.3 | Hysteresis bound (Theorem 7.4) and replay identity of a threshold projection | experiment **E16** of [R02]: revisions per hour with and without hysteresis; replay twice and compare seal digests |
| O7.4 | The engine side of Law 7.5, items 1 and 2 (the law is proved on the model) | tests with retype and removal fixtures: the series survives, the read answers `Undeclared`, a view before the removal shows the samples |
| O7.5 | Production of `ω` from raw samples is reproducible when no ring overflow occurs | replay test from the raw input log |
| O7.6 | Law 7.5 item 3 on the engine (D36): windows combined by the property's rule, no value and a counted conflict for two different values that are not monoid values | test T-MERGE-TELE: a merge per combine rule, rows before and after the link, against the as-if value |
| O7.7 | The engine's `health` graph equals Def. 7.12 (Proposition 7.7 is proved on the model), and Proposition 7.8 with a diagnostic when a chain exceeds the depth | generated topologies and signal histories against a batch roll-up; a runtime diagnostic for a shortest path longer than 16 edges, or `Fixpoint` |
| O7.8 | Def. 7.6 states the late horizon on logical time, and Theorem 6.2 premise B1 becomes a checker rule (one writer per telemetry key) | amend Def. 7.6; a checker test that rejects a telemetry sink whose group key is finer than its element key |

## Ch. 8 Sync direction and bidirectional consistency

Material taken out of the chapter in the reader rewrite (2026-11-12).

### Header of the chapter before the rewrite

**Claim.** The direction in which a rule may be edited is declared per rule (`forward`, `readonly`, `bijective`, `lens`, `sync`). Lens laws survive stacking, and a backward edit changes the internal graph only.
**Status.** Built: `readonly`, `bijective` and `lens` rules, backward edits with routing to the owning rule, a staged forward evaluation and the PutGet check of Theorem 8.5, typed rejections. One lens exists, the capability view of `acme.biz`. Tested on examples and on generated edit sequences for that one lens (8.5). Proved at gate 1a on a model: the laws of a formal `sync` fragment (Def. 8.7, 8.8, Theorem 8.7). Not built: `sync` rules and correspondences, because the engine has no stored correspondence graph and no FWD and BWD translators; the palette lists `sync` as unsupported and a `sync` rule is a typed error. The inverse of the ingestion rule is derived only for one entity sink and one relationship sink over one authored rows port; otherwise the graph is `NotEditable`. Deviation D12.
**Gate.** 1a (theory): direction classes and lens laws. It bears on 1b (what can be edited backwards) without a stated claim.
Uses: Ch. 3 (graph), Ch. 4 (patches, `toPatch`, squash), Ch. 5 (rules, ownership P2, operators), Ch. 6 (denotation, T5, T7).
Used by: Ch. 9, 15.
Sources: [R03] sections 3.3, 4, 8.4; [R01] gap 5, finding 10, section 2.3 (complement attribution), T11, T12; [foster2007], [hofmann2011], [diskin2011], [hermann2013].

The word direction has four meanings in the contracts (finding 10). This chapter concerns **sync direction** only: the direction in which changes of a projection may propagate. Edge orientation, traversal direction (`Traverse.direction`) and flow direction are different things (glossary). A projection need not be bidirectional. Sync direction is declared.

### Evidence with test and file names (old 8.5)

**Evidence from the prototype.** The engine runs this lens (`engine/definitions/acme.biz.sattle`; the `tree` rule of the same module is a second lens rule over `PartOf`, the `counts` rule is `readonly`). What the tests establish:
- *Round trip.* `Create_rename_reparent_and_retire_flow_through_the_master_as_authored_rows`: create, rename, re-parent and retire reach the master as authored rows and come back in the view.
- *GetPut*: putting the unchanged view back leaves the state digest and the master revision unchanged.
- *PutGet and atomic rejection*: 8 seeded groups of 12 random sequences of up to 8 view edits (create, rename, add and delete an edge, delete with and without its edges). Every accepted edit is found in the new view, every rejected one changes nothing. This is the experiment Q3 of [R03] on one lens, with random edits and the engine as reference, not a finite reference model.
- *No PutPut*: create then delete leaves a `retired` capability in the master, while applying only the final view state to the original master leaves none. `putput` is declared false.
- *Rejections*: the codes asserted are `NotEditable`, `ReadOnlyByRule`, `StaleBase`, `PutGetViolation` and `ConstraintViolation`. Deleting a parent while its edges stay in the view is a `PutGetViolation`: the staged check of Theorem 8.5 catches it and no rule does. Deleting it together with its edges is accepted. A cycle and an edge to a retired endpoint are rejected. The other codes of `engine/PROTOCOL.md` (`NotAccepted`, `TelemetryNotEditable`, `AmbiguousKey`, `KeyMismatch`, `BadEdit`) are implemented but not asserted by these tests.
- *Scope*: the master changes only on the authored partition, which is Theorem 8.6 for this instance.
**Not established.** Theorem 8.3 on a chain of more than one lens link, a lens over a `Join`, the ingestion-sequence base of 8.4, activation rule S5 as a separate check, T12. The canvas has no edit UI for data views (`canvas/README.md`: "not built"); the protocol endpoint was exercised by tests, not by the canvas.

## 8.6 Delta to iteration 0.1

- **Gap 5.** Direction is declared by rule class, and per property through ownership. `ProjectionGraphInput` gets no direction mode, because the graph space stays a DAG and backward edits route against its edges without cycles.
- **`BidirectionalRule`.** Replaced by `Rule` with a class. Hand-written backward programs are removed because they cannot be checked against the laws. `LensLaw` is implied by the class. `CorrespondenceTemplate` with complement assignments is removed: identity-aligned lenses over stored sources need no complement, and `sync` rules declare `keep`.
- **`Correspondence`.** A kernel type with derived per-rule subtypes (typed graph data).
- **"Lens complement" index** of ARCHITECTURE.md exists only for `sync` rules and virtual sources.
- **`BackwardEdit` and `BackwardProposal`.** Kept, with a base revision per graph on the path and, proposed here, the ingestion sequence of the master (8.4).
- **S5** is a new activation rule.
- **Complement attribution** (R01 2.3): the contract text credits the source that [R01] lists as G2 ([foster2007]) for a stored complement. In [foster2007] asymmetric lenses take the old source in `put`. The stored-complement form comes from [hofmann2011] (and constant complement from Bancilhon and Spyratos, [bancilhon1981]). Sattle uses the old-source form for stored sources and the complement form for `sync`.
- **Terminology.** The contract's `Direction { Outgoing, Incoming, Either }` is traversal direction. `Port.direction` in [R03] is a port mode (`in`, `out`), not a sync direction.

## 8.7 Deviations found by the prototype

Ids refer to Ch. 14 (14.8).

- **D12.** `sync` rules and edit policies of a sync class are not built, for the reason in the status line. `Join` is not a lens step; its use inside a `readonly` rule is built and does not touch the backward path. `readonly`, `bijective` and `lens` are. The staged PutGet check (Theorem 8.5) is built and rejects with `PutGetViolation`. The base of an edit is the revisions of the graphs on the path (`baseRevisions`), without the ingestion sequence of 8.4. Only one lens link per edit path has been exercised.

| O8.2 | Theorem 8.7 (b) to (d) | Q4 |
| O8.3 | Which joins can be lens steps | Q9: key-preserving n:1 join puts, laws on a pod-to-node join |
